| 497 | Fixed the driver dispatch-readiness gate that could never be satisfied (`getDriverReadiness` returned `{isReady:false, blockerStepNames:[], openInstanceId:null}` — blocked from dispatch with zero actionable steps). Three compounding root causes: (1) no tenant ever received an `ON_DRIVER_CREATE` Auto-Start Rule — `seedStarterPlaybooks` seeded the 3 starter Playbooks but ZERO `PlaybookTrigger` rows, and recipes require manual enablement, so `fireEvent(ON_DRIVER_CREATE)` matched nothing; (2) the one fire-point (carrier driver create route) fired at the wrong moment with the wrong id — DRIVER `PlaybookInstance`s key on the linked `User.id` (via `verifyEntity`), which only exists after invite acceptance, but nothing fired at acceptance, and the create-route fired `carrierDriver.id` (userId null pre-acceptance) → `verifyEntity` NOT_FOUND → no instance; (3) the resolver gated on the stale `User.isDispatchReady` flag (DB default `false`). Fix (3 atomic commits): Task 1 — new pure `deriveDriverReadiness.ts` helper computes readiness from LIVE step instances (open blocker = status∈{NOT_STARTED,IN_PROGRESS,FAILED} ∧ `stepSnapshot.isDispatchBlocker===true`), `isReady` DEFINED as `blockerStepNames.length===0` so `{isReady:false, blockerStepNames:[]}` is impossible by construction; rewrote `getDriverReadiness` to use it (no-instance ⇒ `isReady:true` + optional `warning:'NO_ONBOARDING_INSTANCE'`, read-only), dropped the `User.isDispatchReady` short-circuit, return shape backward-compatible (only optional `warning` added). Task 2 — DRY optional-`tenantPrisma` refactor of `fireEvent`/`generatePlaybookInstance` (default `getTenantPrisma()`, injectable for header-less contexts); `seedStarterPlaybooks` now seeds a default-on `ON_DRIVER_CREATE`→CDL Driver Onboarding trigger (`conditions:{}` match-all, `recurringConfig:{_custom:true}` so it's visible/toggleable in the Auto-Start Rules custom-rules table); fire `ON_DRIVER_CREATE` at invite acceptance (DRIVER role, post-tx, keyed on `user.id`, explicit `getTenantPrismaForOrg` since acceptance is pre-auth); hardened the create-route fire with an explicit client too (de-dupe via generatePlaybookInstance CONFLICT keeps both safe). Task 3 — idempotent backfill script (`scripts/backfill-driver-onboarding-instances.ts`, run manually via `npx tsx --env-file=.env.local`, NOT wired to cron) that per tenant ensures the playbook + default trigger exist and creates onboarding instances for linked drivers missing one. Verified: new `deriveDriverReadiness.test.ts` 5/5 + `seed.test.ts` 6/6 pass; tsc 0 new errors; the 5 `workflows-dispatch-enforcement` failures are a PRE-EXISTING `headers()`-outside-request-scope test-harness issue (confirmed identical at parent commit 512b1a23 — not a regression); backfill compiles, NOT run against live DB. No schema/migration change. Not deployed/pushed | 2026-07-23 | df46f3f6, 45c423a8, a7f451f6 | [497-fix-driver-readiness-gate-that-can-never](./quick/497-fix-driver-readiness-gate-that-can-never/) |
| 496 | Fixed two pre-existing carrier bugs surfaced during quick-495 browser verification. (1) OnboardingTour: `next()` fired `finish()` (markTourSeen server action + setActive) from inside the `setIndex` state-updater, so it ran during the render phase and logged "Cannot update a component (`Router`) while rendering a different component (`OnboardingTourProvider`)". Fixed by moving the finish-vs-advance branch decision into the handler body (using `index` from closure), leaving `setIndex` to only ever return `i + 1`. (2) Grid preferences: `GET /api/user/grid-preferences/[gridId]` returned 404 for authenticated users with no saved prefs — showing a red 404 for every grid on first load AND making `useGridPreferences` treat the user as unauthenticated, so customizations silently saved to localStorage instead of the DB (the DB row was never created; server-side grid-pref persistence never engaged). Fixed by changing the no-preference branch to return 200 `{ preferences: null }` (401 unchanged for unauthenticated), and refactoring `fetchPreferencesFromApi` to return a discriminated `{ authed, prefs }` union so `load()` sets `isAuthenticatedRef.current = true` on any 200 response even when prefs are null — subsequent saves now route to the DB via the existing PUT upsert. Verified: tsc 0 errors (clean baseline, no regressions). | 495 | Fixed the duplicate-form DOM bug on carrier create/edit pages. Root cause: the mobile-web DS pattern rendered BOTH breakpoint variants of a form at once, toggled with CSS only (`lg:hidden <XMobile>` sibling of `hidden lg:block <XForm>`), leaving two complete `<form>` trees with independent, unsynchronized react-hook-form/useState live in the DOM — so a filled contract form's "Create Contract" click bound to the wrong copy and fired ZERO network requests, plus duplicate submit buttons, doubled screen-reader announcements, and 4 driver dropdowns on trips/new. Fix: new SSR-safe `useIsDesktop()` hook (returns `undefined` until mounted → neither variant renders during hydration, no mismatch) + `ResponsiveSwitch` component (mobile/desktop/fallback slots) that mounts EXACTLY ONE variant based on `matchMedia('(min-width: 1024px)')`. Converted 7 create pages (contracts/new, trips/new, loads/new, clients/new, facilities/new, fleet/drivers/new, templates/new) — desktop-only back-link/header chrome moved into each switch's desktop slot, not dropped — plus 5 `[id]` edit pages with a genuine simultaneous-duplicate-form risk (clients/[id], contracts/[id], facilities/[id], templates/[id], loads/[id]). Audited + left unchanged (confirmed safe — desktop edit form gated behind a view-default toggle whose state never syncs to the CSS-hidden mobile variant): fleet/drivers/[id], fleet/trucks/[id], trips/[id]; fleet/trucks/new is single-form. Verified: tsc 0 errors, no regressions. FOLLOW-UP (live browser verify surfaced the TRUE root cause): the per-page fix alone still showed `form.length===2` at desktop because OwnerShell (components/navigation/owner-shell.tsx) rendered `{children}` in BOTH the desktop (hidden lg:flex) and mobile (lg:hidden) chrome frames — so EVERY owner page's content was duplicated in the DOM (confirmed: 4 GET /carrier/clients from ContractForm on one page = 2 instances × StrictMode). Fixed by gating children into exactly one frame via useIsDesktop (desktop frame holds children while breakpoint is undefined/desktop to preserve SSR content; mobile frame only when ===false; mutually exclusive so never both). This shell was the multiplier ABOVE the leaf pages — both fixes needed (shell stops the ×2, page ResponsiveSwitch stops the hidden duplicate dropdowns). Cures all carrier forms at once. Browser-verified live: form.length 2→1 at desktop, 0 at mobile (mobile create has no <form>, submits via onClick), content renders at both breakpoints, AND the original symptom is gone — filling the contract form + clicking Create Contract fired a SINGLE POST /api/v1/carrier/contracts → 201 → redirect → contract created (was zero requests before). Note: the local /api/v1/carrier/clients 500s seen during verification were a stale dev-server Prisma client (see [[project-stale-dev-prisma-client-500]]), fixed by restarting next dev — not a code bug. Not deployed/pushed | 2026-07-23 | 4134038f, 38d99321, ae70bb6d, 2421a7a2 | [495-fix-duplicate-form-dom-bug-on-carrier-cr](./quick/495-fix-duplicate-form-dom-bug-on-carrier-cr/) |

Root cause: components/auth/freight-map.tsx draws US state outlines via d3-geo geoAlbersUsa, a trigonometric projection (Math.sin/cos/sqrt) whose last-ULP results differ between Node's V8 (server) and the browser's V8, so the SSR'd SVG path `d` strings didn't byte-match the client → hydration mismatch (shared component, both auth pages affected). Fix: render the decorative <svg> client-only — added a `mounted` flag (useEffect(()=>setMounted(true),[])) gating the SVG; the container <div ref> still SSRs for getBoundingClientRect measurement, but the mismatching paths never SSR. No visible regression (map is aria-hidden and fades in). | DI-9W | **Document Import Phase 9-web — the driver inspection and trip-start gate on the WEB portal (spec Section 12).** Drivers use web, not `apps/mobile`; Phase 9 (ec31b515) shipped this on RN only. **The shaping constraint: escaping `(driver)/layout.tsx` chrome also escapes its auth**, so a sibling `(driver-fullscreen)/` group re-establishes the DRIVER role check and `resolveInspectionAccess` adds the ownership question a layout cannot ask — called by both pages AND all seven server actions, because a stale tab never re-runs a layout. Full-screen walkaround: section per screen, progress bar, pass/fail/**N-A** at 56px, note required (server-side now, not just one RN component), **photo PUT to R2 at capture**, free back navigation, in-house `<canvas>` + pointer-events signature with name and ISO timestamp. Blocked screen and both `alert()` refusals replaced by a dialog carrying the gate sentence and routing on `code`. **Two pre-existing web defects fixed**: the fail path wrote `notes`/`photoPath` where every Phase 9 reader reads `note`/`photoUrls` (so the blocked screen showed failures with no note and no photo), and it bypassed `failInspectionItem` entirely — Section 12 dispatcher notification never fired on web. `FAIL_NOTE_MIN_LENGTH` 3 → 8 (3 was not deliberate; accepted "abc"). `requiresPhotoOnFail` declared canonical and `failInspectionItem` realigned onto the shared reader — seeded tenants now genuinely enforce the photo the seed always asked for. ONLINE-ONLY by decision, no queue, no false success. tsc probed and clean in both apps; suite byte-identical to fdc005d8 (66 pre-existing failures, zero regressions). **Guard test written against real rows but its 6 DB cases are UNRUN — only DATABASE_URL available is production.** | 2026-08-25 | a240e466 | [09-WEB-SUMMARY.md](./document-import/09-WEB-SUMMARY.md) |
| 543 | **Make inspection playbooks work, then let the gate see them.** quick-542 found the gate could not see the demo tenant's inspections (category SAFETY, gate wants VEHICLE_INSPECTION); fixing that alone would have exposed three defects sitting behind it. In order: (1) the photo key has THREE live spellings — `requiresPhotoOnFail` (176 templates), `require_photo_on_fail` (35), `requiresPhoto` (0) — now read by one function in **precedence** order (first key PRESENT wins, even when false; verified `multi_key = 0` before choosing), plus the rule that prevents a fourth: **one exported constant for writers, one exported reader for readers, neither ever spells the key inline** — applied to `seedStarterPlaybooks`, not merely written down. (2) Non-DRIVER steps render read-only and named ("dispatch completes this one") and are **excluded from the progress denominator**, decided from the assignment COLUMNS because that is what `findOwnStep` reads. (3) **FINDING: no control exists anywhere to mark a step dispatch-blocking** — absent from `addStepSchema` AND `updatePlaybookStepSchema`, Prisma default false, so `blockTripStartOnFailedInspection` is inert on every self-built checklist, and Phase 9 item 3's non-critical branch has never been exercisable by data either way (the seed sets it true on every step; the builder cannot set it at all). (4) `VEHICLE_INSPECTION` added to the builder dropdown + the three PlaybookCard maps. (5) 8 SAFETY/VEHICLE playbooks migrated across 7 internal tenants, rows listed before the write and RETURNING confirmed the same 8; "Vehicle Check" (SAFETY/**DRIVER**, 0 steps) correctly excluded — had it been swept in it would have become that tenant's oldest VEHICLE_INSPECTION playbook and handed its drivers an empty walkaround. (6) Gate lookup and step arithmetic verified against live data (v2: 4 gate-counted = 4 driver-answerable, the FORM_FILL excluded from both); a signature screen demanding ink on a playbook with no SIGNATURE step was found during verification and fixed. **Also corrected quick-542 §6.3**, which claimed the FORM_FILL made the checklist uncompletable — `buildSnapshot` has always filtered to INSPECTION_ITEM, so it never reached the gate; the real defect was a dead control and a stuck progress bar. tsc probed and clean in both apps; suite byte-identical to ab45f571. **Not walked in a browser** — needs driver credentials against production. | 2026-08-25 | b00695de | [543-make-inspection-playbooks-work](./quick/543-make-inspection-playbooks-work/) |
| 544 | **Give owners a way to mark an inspection step dispatch-blocking.** quick-543 item 3 found `isDispatchBlocker` had no control anywhere; this closes it. **The gap was in the middle of a 9-link path** — the column, `buildStepSnapshot`, `computeDispatchReadiness` and the Phase 9 gate all worked, and `PlaybookStepItem` already carried the value to the client, so the plumbing looked complete from both ends. The three silent links were `addStepSchema`, `updatePlaybookStepSchema` and the two mutations behind them; only `seedStarterPlaybooks` (and `duplicate`, which copies) could ever produce a blocking step. Added to both schemas — `default(false)` on create, **no default on update** (a partial update with a false default would silently clear the flag for any caller omitting it), and both mutations now write it behind the same `!== undefined` guard as their neighbours. **Control copy avoids the column's name entirely**: label is conditional — "Failing this stops the trip" on INSPECTION_ITEM, "Must be finished before dispatch" elsewhere — plus two caveats an owner cannot otherwise see (blocking only bites while `blockTripStartOnFailedInspection` is on; `buildStepSnapshot` immutability means it applies to future runs only). **Offered on every stepType, not just inspections**: 192 of the 384 existing blocking steps are in ONBOARDING/PARTNER playbooks, so restricting it would leave half the field's real usage uneditable and misrepresent what it means. Amber "Blocks dispatch" badge on the step row so it is auditable without opening eight editors. **Reported, not built:** where to surface an all-non-blocking playbook (recommend builder header + Operations settings together). **Reported, not migrated:** 156 non-blocking steps of 540 across 23 tenants; the actionable subset is 8 inspection playbooks with zero blockers — the same 8 from the 2026-04-24 script — of which **only DriveCommand Demo is live-armed today**. tsc probed clean in both apps; suite byte-identical to bde19ea3. Not browser-tested. | 2026-08-25 | 2bc2e499 | [544-dispatch-blocking-control](./quick/544-dispatch-blocking-control/) |
| 545 | **Surface inspection checklists that block nothing while blocking is on.** quick-544's step-5 recommendation, built on both surfaces it named. **The condition is five clauses and every one is load-bearing**: both tenant settings on; an active, non-deleted VEHICLE_INSPECTION playbook with `entityType IN ('DISPATCH','VEHICLE')` — a **two-value filter, not `VEHICLE`**, since the seeded DVIR shape is DISPATCH; **at least one INSPECTION_ITEM step**, because with zero the gate answers INSPECTION_REQUIRED forever and drivers dead-end, a *stricter* failure that "nothing can block a trip" would misdescribe; and none of those items blocking. A blocking SIGNATURE or DOCUMENT_UPLOAD does **not** count — `criticalFailures` is built only from INSPECTION_ITEM outcomes. **Clause 5 is evaluated across every candidate playbook, not "the one that will run"**: `ensureTripInspection`'s oldest-first pick is only the fallback and a trigger can spawn any playbook, so requiring all of them to be toothless is the reading that can never fire while protection exists — a knowingly-accepted false negative on a mixed tenant, of which production has none. One shared pure predicate (`inspection-coverage.ts`) drives both surfaces so they cannot drift; the settings line reads both toggles from **live** state so it appears on flip, not on save, and is mutually exclusive with the existing "Inspections are off" line by construction. Both messages end with the literal toggle label "Failing this stops the trip". **Correction to the brief: `DriveCommand Demo` was switched to `requirePreTripInspection = false` at 08:42 UTC the same day, so NO tenant satisfies clause 1 today — the gap is latent across all 8 playbooks / 7 tenants, not live in one.** **Reported, not fixed:** `findTripInspection` queries only `entityType='DISPATCH'` while `ensureTripInspection` can create a VEHICLE-scoped instance, so on those 7 tenants the instance it creates is invisible to the current-inspection lookup and the gate clears via the PRIOR_INSPECTION rung. **Reported, not built (step 6): the same gap class DOES exist for driver ONBOARDING and `User.isDispatchReady`, and its enforcement is unconditional** — `trips.ts:216` throws DRIVER_NOT_DISPATCH_READY with no tenant toggle, so a zero-blocker onboarding checklist marks every driver ready and the dispatcher sees a green light that means nothing; 7 of 23 active ONBOARDING playbooks are affected. Nothing auto-fixed, migrated or defaulted to blocking. `evaluateTripStartGate`, `computeDispatchReadiness` and `buildStepSnapshot` untouched; no DDL, no data write. tsc PROBED in both apps (the mobile probe initially created a stray `lib/utils.ts` — deleted, redone in a tracked file, tree restored byte-for-byte); suite failure set byte-identical to e5232725, +11 passing from the new tests. Not browser-tested. | 2026-08-25 | 73466b01 | [545-surface-checklists-that-block-nothing-wh](./quick/545-surface-checklists-that-block-nothing-wh/) |

Last session: 2026-06-16T16:07:00Z
